Home › Guide › Marbella Fashion Week: Yacht Charters for Press Day, Shows & After-Parties By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella Fashion Week transforms Puerto Banús into a hub of style, glamour and exclusive events. From Press Day previews to runway shows and late-night after-parties, the city buzzes with energy. A Marbella fashion week yacht charter offers a unique way to experience it all — with privacy, luxury and a front-row seat on the water. What is Marbella Fashion Week? Marbella Fashion Week is a biannual event showcasing Spanish and international designers. It typically runs for 3–5 days in late spring and early autumn. The programme includes Press Day (for media and influencers), runway shows at venues along the Golden Mile and Puerto Banús, and exclusive after-parties at beach clubs, private villas and superyachts. Press Day on a Yacht Press Day is the quietest and most strategic part of the week. Brands present their collections to editors, bloggers and stylists. Instead of a crowded hotel suite, charter a yacht for a relaxed, photogenic setting. The Boat Rental Marbella fleet includes the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) and Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) — perfect for intimate gatherings with drinks, snacks and sea views. Both depart from Puerto Banús, minutes from the main venues. Runway Shows from the Water Some runway shows take place on the Puerto Banús promenade or at beach clubs like Ocean Club. A yacht anchored nearby gives you an unobstructed view of the catwalk without the crowds. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) offers a raised flybridge deck for optimal sightlines. You can watch the show, then cruise along the coast to Cala del Faro for a swim. After-Parties on Superyachts After the runway, the party moves to the marina. The biggest after-parties happen on superyachts. Our Marbella fashion week yacht options for parties include: Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) — €4,719 minimum 4 hours, includes a jet ski. TITLE: Marbella Feria San Bernabé: How to Combine the Casetas With a Daytime Cruise
DESCRIPTION: Combine Marbella feria San Bernabé casetas with a daytime yacht cruise. Charter from Puerto Banús from €749/2h. Book your boat now.

Home › Guide › Marbella Feria San Bernabé: How to Combine the Casetas With a Daytime Cruise By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella feria San Bernabé is the city’s biggest summer party — six days of casetas, fairground rides, and live music from 11 to 16 June. But you don’t have to spend every hour on dry land. A daytime yacht charter from Puerto Banús lets you escape the midday heat, cruise the Costa del Sol, and still make it back for the evening festivities. Here’s how to combine both without rushing. Why Add a Yacht to Your Feria San Bernabé Plan? The casetas open from lunchtime, but the real buzz starts after 20:00. That leaves you a solid window for a morning or early afternoon cruise. The Mediterranean in June averages 24°C water temperature and 28°C air — perfect for swimming, sunbathing, and sipping cava offshore. A boat also gives you a different perspective of Marbella’s coastline, from the Golden Mile to Cabopino. Plus, anchoring off Marbella’s main beach puts you right opposite the fairground. You can watch the lights come on at dusk and hear the music drifting across the water. It’s a memorable way to start your evening. Best Boat Options for a Feria Day Cruise Our fleet departs from Puerto Banús, a 10-minute drive from the feria site. For small groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) or Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) start at €749 for two hours, all inclusive. That’s enough time to cruise to Cala del Faro, drop anchor for a swim, and return. If you want more space and a jet ski, the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) is available from €4,719 for four hours, with a jet ski included. For larger parties, the Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) or Canados 86 (26m, 12 pax) can be booked on request — contact us via WhatsApp for pricing. All charters include a skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT. No extras. Just bring sunscreen and swimwear. Suggested Itinerary: Cruise + Casetas 10:00–14:00 — Depart Puerto Banús. Head east along the coast past Marbella Marina, anchor off the feria beach for a swim. Lunch on board (bring your own or order from a beach club). Return by 14:00. 14:00–20:00 — Free time. Shower, rest, or explore Marbella’s old town. The casetas start serving food and drink from midday, so you can grab lunch at the fair if you prefer. 20:00–late — Head to the feria. Wear white and red (the feria colours). Dance sevillanas, try churros, and ride the Ferris wheel. Taxi back to Puerto Banús or your hotel. Alternative: book a 12:00–16:00 slot if you prefer a later start. Where to Anchor Near the Feria The best spot is off Playa de la Venus or Playa del Faro, directly in front of the fairground. Depth is 5–8 metres, sandy bottom. You can drop anchor and swim ashore (the beach is public) or stay on board and watch the action from the water. The sunset view over La Concha mountain is spectacular. For a quieter swim, head west to Cala Cortés or east to Cabopino — both 15–20 minutes from Puerto Banús. What to Bring and Wear Pack a small bag: swimsuit, towel, sunglasses, sunscreen (SPF 50+), a light jacket for the evening, and your phone for photos. The boat provides cold drinks and snacks, but you can bring your own food. Remember cash for the casetas — some stalls don’t accept cards. For the feria, women often wear flamenco dresses (trajes de gitana), men wear white shirts and red scarves. But casual summer wear is fine too. Booking Tips for Feria Week Feria San Bernabé is the busiest week in Marbella. Yachts get booked a month in advance. We recommend reserving at least one week ahead. Payment is by bank transfer or card. WhatsApp us for availability on specific dates and boats. If you’re flexible, the Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) and Pershing 46 (14m, 10 pax) are also popular choices — pricing on request. For more ideas, see our Marbella boat rental guide . Frequently asked questions What is the Marbella feria San Bernabé? It’s Marbella’s annual summer fair, held 11–16 June, honouring the city’s patron saint. The main site on Avenida del Mar has dozens of casetas (marquee tents), fairground rides, live flamenco, and late-night bars. It’s free to enter, and locals dress in flamenco attire. How far is Puerto Banús from the feria? Puerto Banús is about 6 km west of Marbella city centre — a 10-minute taxi ride or 20-minute drive. Plan for €15–20 by cab. The feria is held near the beach at Avenida del Mar, so it’s easy to combine a cruise and the fair. What time should I book a yacht to still enjoy the feria? Book a half-day charter from 10:00 to 14:00 or 12:00 to 16:00. You’ll be back at Puerto Banús by late afternoon. Shower at your hotel or villa, then head to the casetas around 20:00. That gives you four hours of boat time and the full evening at the feria. Which boats are best for a daytime cruise during the feria? For small groups (up to 9), the Astondoa 40 at €749/2h is a great value. For larger parties up to 12, the Mangusta 80 at €4,719/4h includes a jet ski. Both depart from Puerto Banús and include skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks. Can I anchor near the feria beach and watch from the water? Yes. The feria is held along Avenida del Mar, right by the beach. You can anchor off Marbella’s main beach and see the fairground lights and hear music from the water. It’s a popular spot for sunset mooring before heading ashore. What’s included in the charter price? All charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). No hidden costs. Tier B boats also include a jet ski. Pricing on request for other models. Do I need to book the yacht in advance for feria week? Yes. Feria San Bernabé is one of Marbella’s busiest weeks. Boats book up fast. Reserve at least one week ahead. Payment is via bank transfer or card. Here's exactly what you'll pay and what you'll get. Marbella Fishing Charter Cost by Boat Size Our fleet in Puerto Banús covers everything from nimble speedboats to superyachts. For fishing, the sweet spot is our Tier A boats: Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) – €749/2h, €1,299/4h, €2,299/8h Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) – same pricing as above These are sport-cruiser style boats with good deck space, a bimini for shade, and a competent skipper who knows the local grounds. Typical fishing grounds include: Cala del Faro – rocky bottom, good for bream and sea bass Cala Cortés – sheltered bay, ideal for families Cabopino – reef structures holding dentex and amberjack Estepona coast – deeper water for larger pelagics Your skipper will choose based on weather, tides, and what's biting. Most trips stay within 5–10 nautical miles of the coast, so you'll never be far from shore. How to Save on Your Fishing Charter The best value is the 8-hour full-day charter at €2,299 – that's €287 per hour for a private boat with 9 guests, or about €255 per person for a group of 9. Compare that to a shared public fishing trip, and you get privacy, flexibility, and a better boat. For smaller groups (up to 5 pax), consider our Dubhe (8m) or Mariah SX21 (6.4m). These are smaller but perfectly capable for inshore fishing, and pricing is typically lower – contact us via WhatsApp for a quote. Book mid-week (Monday–Thursday) and you'll often have more flexibility on timing. Peak season (June–August) weekends fill fast, so book at least a week ahead. What Species Can You Catch? Marbella's Mediterranean waters offer a mix of species depending on season: Spring/Autumn: sea bass, red mullet, common bream Summer: bluefish, bonito, mackerel, horse mackerel Winter: dentex, amberjack, greater weever Your skipper will provide the right rigs and bait. Most charters use a mix of bottom fishing and trolling. Catch-and-release is encouraged for undersized fish. Booking Process and Cancellation Policy To book, simply contact us via WhatsApp with your preferred date, group size, and duration. We'll confirm availability and send a payment link (secure card or bank transfer). A 50% deposit secures the booking; the balance is due 7 days before departure. Cancellations are free up to 48 hours before departure. Within 48 hours, we'll try to reschedule – if that's not possible, the deposit is non-refundable. Weather cancellations are always free. Yes, basic fishing rods, reels, and tackle are provided on all our charters. The crew will clean and bag your catch for you. There are no additional fees for keeping fish, but please check local size and bag limits – your skipper will advise on what's legal and sustainable. Are there cheaper options for a smaller group? For groups of up to 5, consider our Dubhe (8m) or Mariah SX21 (6.4m). Pricing is on request via WhatsApp, but they tend to be more affordable than the larger yachts. They also depart from Puerto Banús. What's the best time of year for fishing in Marbella? Year-round is good, but peak season runs May to October when water temperatures hit 22–26°C. You'll target species like sea bass, bream, and bluefish. Winter brings larger dentex and amberjack. Your skipper will adapt the plan to conditions. Do I need a fishing license? No. The charter's skipper holds a commercial fishing licence that covers all passengers. You don't need to bring anything except sunscreen, a hat, and your camera. Can I book a private fishing charter for a special occasion? Yes, all our charters are private – you won't share the boat with strangers. We can add catering, a photographer, or even a barbecue on board. Bluefin tuna migrate through the Strait of Gibraltar from June to October. The peak months are August and September, when water temperatures reach 22–24°C. Early morning and late afternoon charters yield the best results. The 2031 season is expected to follow the same pattern, with high catch rates in the summer. TITLE: Tuna Fishing Season in Marbella 2042: Best Months for a Charter
DESCRIPTION: Plan your 2042 tuna fishing season Marbella charter. Best months, boat options from Puerto Banús, and what&#x27;s included. Book your trip now.

Home › Guide › Tuna Fishing Season in Marbella 2042: Best Months for a Charter By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella's tuna fishing season in 2042 promises exceptional sport fishing for bluefin tuna, with peak months from June to September. Departing from Puerto Banús, our charter fleet offers boats from 8m to 29m, all with experienced skippers and full amenities. Whether you're after a trophy bluefin or a family day at sea, this guide covers the best months, boat options, and what's included in your Marbella boat charter . Peak Tuna Fishing Season in Marbella 2042 The prime months for tuna fishing off Marbella are June through September 2042. Water temperatures reach 24-26°C, attracting large bluefin tuna migrating through the Strait of Gibraltar. July and August are the busiest, with average catches of 100-200 kg specimens. Early morning (6-10 AM) and late afternoon (4-8 PM) are the most productive times. September offers slightly cooler conditions but still excellent action, especially for those seeking fewer crowds. Best Boats for Tuna Fishing from Puerto Banús Our fleet includes several vessels ideal for offshore tuna fishing. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) provides a stable platform with ample deck space. The Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) offers speed and comfort for reaching distant grounds. For smaller groups, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) is a cost-effective option. All boats come with a licensed skipper who knows the local hotspots like the Banco de la Concha and Estepona seamounts. What's Included in Your Fishing Charter Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). Fishing gear is not provided, but we can arrange rod and reel rentals upon request. Our skippers are experienced in tuna fishing techniques, including trolling with lures and live bait. Catch-and-release is encouraged to support sustainable fisheries. Pricing for Tuna Fishing Charters Our Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) start at €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours. Tier B boats (Mangusta 80, white or grey) start at €4,719 for a minimum 4-hour charter, which includes a free jet ski. All other boats are priced on request via WhatsApp. Prices include skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and VAT. No hidden fees. Where to Fish: Top Spots Near Marbella The best tuna fishing grounds are 10-20 nautical miles offshore from Puerto Banús. Key areas include the Banco de la Concha, a underwater bank near Estepona, and the seamounts off Cala del Faro. These spots hold bluefin tuna feeding on sardines and mackerel. Your skipper will navigate based on real-time conditions. For a shorter trip, the waters off Cabopino and Sotogrande also produce tuna during peak season. Tips for a Successful Tuna Fishing Trip Book your charter early, especially for July and August. Wear sun protection (SPF 50+, hat, sunglasses) and bring a windbreaker. Arrive at Puerto Banús 15 minutes before departure. Inform your skipper of any dietary restrictions for snacks. Consider a full-day charter (8 hours) for the best chance of landing a trophy. For more information on our fleet and bookings, check our Marbella boat charter . Frequently asked questions When is the best time for tuna fishing in Marbella in 2042? The peak tuna fishing season in Marbella runs from June to September 2042. Bluefin tuna are most active in July and August, with water temperatures around 24-26°C. Early mornings (6-10 AM) and late afternoons (4-8 PM) typically yield the best catches. What types of tuna can I catch off Marbella? The deep waters off Cabopino and Cala del Faro hold tuna from early summer through late autumn. Puerto Banús gives you direct access to these grounds, with no long transit times. In 2046, the season is expected to follow the same pattern, with early arrivals in June and the heaviest fish in September. Tuna fishing in Marbella typically uses trolling with artificial lures or live bait. Skippers deploy rigged ballyhoo, squid skirts, or poppers to attract surface‑feeding fish. For larger bluefin, stand‑up rod and reel combos with 80–130 lb class gear are standard. If you prefer a more active approach, spinning with heavy poppers works well when fish are breaking water. URL: https://boatrentalinmarbella.com/blog/marbella-half-day-morning-vs-afternoon/
TITLE: Half-Day Charter Marbella: Morning vs Afternoon
DESCRIPTION: Deciding between a Marbella half day morning vs afternoon charter? Compare sea conditions, light, dining, and sunset options for your 4-hour boat trip from Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › Half-Day Charter Marbella: Morning vs Afternoon By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Choosing between a morning and afternoon half-day charter in Marbella can shape your entire experience on the water. Both slots offer distinct advantages—from calm seas and cooler air in the morning to golden light and warmer water in the afternoon. Whether you're planning a family outing, a group celebration, or a romantic escape, knowing the differences helps you book the right 4-hour window. Below, we break down the key factors to consider when deciding on your Marbella half day morning vs afternoon charter. Morning Half-Day Charter (10:00–14:00) Morning charters depart from Puerto Banús at 10:00 and return by 14:00. The sea is typically flat, the wind is light, and the coastline is quieter. This slot is ideal for guests who want a smooth ride, cooler temperatures, and the option to anchor for a swim or a picnic lunch at a cala like Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés . You'll have the coves mostly to yourself, and the light is excellent for photography without the harsh midday glare. A morning charter also leaves your afternoon free for other activities—shopping on the Golden Mile or lunch at a chiringuito in Cabopino . Afternoon Half-Day Charter (15:00–19:00) An afternoon half-day starts at 15:00 and finishes around 19:00, just as the sun begins to set. The water is warmer, making swimming more comfortable, and the later hours offer the famous Marbella sunset behind La Concha mountain. This slot is popular for groups wanting a lively atmosphere, with more boats anchored near beach clubs and coves. You can anchor off Estepona or Sotogrande for a swim, then enjoy cava and snacks as the sky turns orange. The return to Puerto Banús is often the highlight, with the marina lights reflecting on the water. Sea Conditions and Weather Mornings in Marbella are usually calm, with sea breezes picking up after noon. If you or your guests are prone to seasickness, a morning charter is the safer bet. Afternoons can have a light chop, but it's rarely uncomfortable and often welcome for cooling off. Summer temperatures range from 25°C in the morning to 30°C+ in the afternoon—bring sunscreen regardless. Swimming and Water Activities Water temperature is warmer in the afternoon (often 24–26°C in summer) compared to morning (22–24°C). If swimming is a priority, book the afternoon. Mornings are still perfectly swimmable, but the cooler water can be a shock. Both slots allow time for snorkelling, paddleboarding, or using the jet ski included on the Mangusta 80 (Tier B). Dining Options: Onboard or Ashore On a morning charter, you can anchor for a picnic lunch or dock at a beachfront restaurant in Cabopino or Marbella Marina . Afternoon charters work well for a late lunch onboard (15:00–16:00) followed by a sunset drink. Both include drinks and snacks—beer, cava, white wine, soft drinks, and water—so you can focus on the experience. Sunset and Photography If sunset is your priority, choose the afternoon slot. The golden hour from 18:00 to 19:00 provides stunning light for photos, especially with La Concha or the Strait of Gibraltar in the background. Morning light is crisp and clean, ideal for capturing the coastline without haze. Which Boats Are Available? Our fleet offers several options for half-day charters, all departing from Puerto Banús . Tier A boats— Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) and Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax)—cost €749/2h or €2,299/8h. Tier B boats like the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) start at €4,719/4h and include a jet ski. For other boats (e.g., Ferretti 94 , Canados 86 , Pershing 46 ), pricing is on request via WhatsApp. All charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT. Frequently asked questions What is the difference between a morning and afternoon half-day charter in Marbella? A morning half-day runs from 10:00 to 14:00, offering calm seas, cooler temperatures, and a quieter coastline. An afternoon half-day runs from 15:00 to 19:00, featuring warmer water for swimming, golden light, and the chance to watch the sunset over the Mediterranean. Which half-day is better for swimming: morning or afternoon? The afternoon is better for swimming because the sea has warmed up from the morning sun, and the air temperature is at its peak. Morning water is cooler, but still pleasant in summer (typically 22–24°C). Can I have lunch on a morning charter? Yes, you can bring your own picnic or arrange a catered lunch. Many guests anchor at Cala del Faro or Cabopino and enjoy a relaxed onboard meal. Alternatively, you can dock at a chiringuito for a sit-down lunch. Is the afternoon charter better for sunset views? Absolutely. An afternoon charter ending around 19:00 in summer lets you watch the sun dip behind La Concha mountain and the Strait of Gibraltar. It’s a popular time for photos and a glass of cava on the bow. Are sea conditions different between morning and afternoon? Yes. Mornings typically have flat seas and light winds, ideal for smooth cruising. Afternoons can see a light thermal breeze and slight chop, but this is rarely uncomfortable and often welcomed for cooling off. Which time slot is less crowded on the water? Morning charters are quieter, with fewer boats and less activity in coves like Cala Cortés. Afternoons are busier, especially near popular anchorages and beach clubs, but the lively atmosphere is part of the appeal. Can I combine a half-day charter with a beach club visit? Yes, especially in the afternoon. You can anchor near a beach club like Ocean Club Marbella or Playa Padre, use the tender to go ashore, and return to your boat for a drink. Morning charters are better for a quiet swim and snorkel. What boats are available for a half-day charter? Half-day trips (2–4 hours) are perfect for a quick celebration, sunset cruise, or a taster session. Full-day charters (6–8 hours) unlock the coastline all the way to Estepona or Sotogrande, with multiple swim stops and a leisurely lunch. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) and Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) are popular for full-day charters with multiple stops. Weather and Season Considerations In summer (June–September), sea temperatures average 24–26°C and winds are light in the morning. A half-day from 10:00 to 14:00 avoids the afternoon heat. A full-day from 10:00 to 18:00 lets you enjoy the calm morning, a lunch stop, and an afternoon swim. In spring or autumn, a half-day is often enough — the sun sets earlier and the water is cooler (18–22°C). What’s Included in Every Charter? When to Go: Best Season for Jet Skiing The Mediterranean water temperature in Marbella ranges from 18°C in May to 26°C in August. Safety & Licensing Requirements Spanish law requires a jet ski licence (PER or PNB with jet ski endorsement) to ride independently. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter VAT &amp; Tax: What You&#x27;ll Pay
DESCRIPTION: Understand marbella yacht charter VAT tax — Spanish IVA is always included in our prices. No hidden fees, 21% tax covered. Book with total transparency.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter VAT & Tax: What You'll Pay By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers When you're planning a Marbella yacht charter , the last thing you want is a surprise tax bill at the end. That's why every price on our site includes Spanish IVA (VAT) at 21% — no hidden fees, no asterisks. Whether you're booking the €749/2h Astondoa 40 or the €4,719/4h Mangusta 80, what you see is exactly what you pay. This page explains how VAT works on yacht charters in Marbella, so you can book with total confidence. Spanish IVA on Yacht Charters: The Basics In Spain, all recreational boat charters are subject to a 21% Value Added Tax (IVA). This applies to any private charter departing from a Spanish port, including Puerto Banús, Marbella Marina, and Cabopino. The tax is calculated on the total charter fee, including the boat, skipper, fuel, and any included extras like drinks and snacks. Our prices are always quoted inclusive of IVA, so you don't need to do any mental arithmetic. For example, the €749/2h rate for the Azimut 39 already has the 21% tax baked in. This transparency is part of our commitment to honest, no-fluff service. What's Included in Our VAT-Inclusive Prices Every charter with us includes the following, all covered by the 21% IVA: Licensed skipper Fuel for the entire trip Water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, and cava Light snacks Insurance and safety equipment Spanish IVA (21%) — already included There are no additional port fees, cleaning charges, or tourist taxes. The price you see on our Marbella boat rental page is the final price. How IVA Affects Different Boat Tiers Our fleet is divided into pricing tiers, and IVA is included across all of them: Boat Price (IVA included) Duration Astondoa 40 €749 2 hours Azimut 39 €749 2 hours Mangusta 80 (any variant) €4,719 4 hours (min.) Other boats (e.g., Pershing 46, Ferretti 94) On request On request For boats like the Ferretti 94 or Canados 86, pricing is customised based on your itinerary. We'll provide a full quote via WhatsApp that includes IVA, so there are no surprises. Can You Reclaim IVA on a Yacht Charter? If you're an EU business with a valid VAT number, you may be able to reclaim the 21% IVA under Spanish reverse-charge rules. This typically applies to charters used for corporate entertainment or client meetings. Non-EU residents generally cannot reclaim IVA, as it's a consumption tax. We recommend speaking with your tax advisor to confirm eligibility. If you need an invoice with your VAT number, just let us know when booking via WhatsApp — we'll provide a proper tax receipt. No Tourist Tax or Hidden Surcharges Unlike some holiday destinations (e.g., the Balearic Islands), Marbella and the Costa del Sol do not impose a tourist tax on yacht charters. There are no municipal surcharges, port entry fees, or environmental taxes added to our prices. The 21% IVA is the only tax you'll pay. This makes chartering from Puerto Banús particularly straightforward. You can focus on enjoying the coastline — from the Golden Mile to Cala del Faro — without worrying about extra costs. What About Charters for Special Events? Whether you're booking a Marbella yacht charter for a birthday party, wedding reception, or corporate event, the same IVA rules apply. Our all-inclusive pricing covers everything, and we can tailor the catering, decorations, or entertainment to your needs. Just ask for a custom quote — it will always include IVA. For example, a 4-hour charter on the Mangusta 80 with a jet ski included costs €4,719, all taxes paid. No extra line items. Why We Include IVA in Our Prices We've been operating in Marbella long enough to know that hidden fees erode trust. By including IVA in every advertised price, we make budgeting simple. You don't need to calculate 21% on top of the quote — it's already there. This approach is consistent across our entire fleet, from the compact Dubhe (€ on request) to the 29m Ferretti 94. It's part of our ethos: honest, direct, and transparent. If you have any questions about taxes, just contact us — we're happy to explain. What is the VAT rate for yacht charters in Marbella? The standard Spanish IVA rate for recreational boat charters is 21%. This applies to all private charters departing from Puerto Banús and other Marbella marinas. Can I reclaim the VAT on my yacht charter? If you are an EU business with a valid VAT number, you may be able to reclaim the IVA under Spanish reverse-charge rules. Non-EU residents generally cannot reclaim. No. Fuel and a licensed skipper are included in every charter, and both are covered by the 21% IVA. You won't pay a cent more than the advertised price. Home › Guide › Cala de Mijas Music Festival: Yacht Charter from Marbella By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Cala de Mijas Music Festival turns a quiet stretch of Costa del Sol beach into one of the busiest weekends of the Andalucian summer — and the easiest way to attend without traffic, parking battles or queues is to arrive by sea. From Puerto Banús the run is roughly 22 nautical miles east, an hour at cruising speed, and you finish the day anchored offshore with the headline act drifting across the water. This guide explains how the trip actually works on our fleet, what it costs, and which boats suit the festival best. For the full boat list and pricing, see the main page . Why arrive at Cala de Mijas by yacht The festival site sits on the beachfront just east of La Cala de Mijas marina. On festival weekend the AP-7 exit backs up from late afternoon, taxis triple their fares and parking within 2 km is gone by 18:00. By yacht you bypass all of it: you anchor 200–300 metres off the beach, tender in when you want and tender out when you've had enough. You keep your own food, drinks and bathroom on board, and the return trip to Puerto Banús in flat night seas is genuinely one of the better parts of the day. Practically speaking, this is a charter that suits groups of 6–12 who want a proper day out rather than just transport. The boat is the venue; the festival is a 90-minute interval in the middle. The route from Puerto Banús All our boats depart Puerto Banús. The eastbound route runs past Marbella Marina, the Golden Mile, Río Verde, Cabopino and Calahonda before reaching La Cala de Mijas. Total distance is around 22 NM. A typical timeline on the Azimut 39 or Astondoa 40 at 22 knots: Time Location Notes 14:00 Depart Puerto Banús Welcome drinks, briefing 14:45 Cabopino Swim stop, 30–45 min 16:30 Arrive Cala de Mijas Anchor, tender check 17:00–22:00 Festival Tender shuttles ashore 22:30 Depart for Banús Dinner on deck 00:00 Back in Banús Late return possible The Mangusta 80 and Pershing 46 run the route noticeably faster, useful if you want a longer Cabopino stop. The Lagoon 380 catamaran is slower but the most stable platform at anchor off an open beach. Boats that suit the festival For groups of 8–11 the sweet spot is the Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 — Tier A pricing, full inclusions, and enough deck space to actually enjoy the day. Both are 12.5m, both seat 9–11 guests, and both come in at €749 for 2 hours rising to €2,299 for 8 hours with skipper, fuel, drinks and 21% IVA included. For a statement event or a 12-guest party, the Mangusta 80 (white or grey) is the obvious upgrade: 24 metres, free jet ski, €4,719 minimum 4 hours. The Azimut 58 Flybridge, Pershing 46 and Ferretti 94 are priced on request via WhatsApp — useful if you want a flybridge for the sound system or extra cabins for guests staying late. For groups that prioritise stability over speed, the Lagoon 380 catamaran (11.6m, 10 pax) sits flat at anchor even in 1-metre swell. That matters at Cala de Mijas, which is an open roadstead. Anchoring and tender logistics The bay east of La Cala marina has a sand seabed in 6–10 metres, holding well in settled weather. We typically anchor 200–300 metres offshore — close enough for a 5-minute tender run, far enough to stay out of the swimming zone. The skipper runs a fixed shuttle: confirmed drop times (e.g. 17:00 and 19:30) and confirmed pickup times (e.g. 22:00 and midnight). You don't need to call — you just turn up at the agreed spot. Conditions matter. Westerly poniente winds above 15 knots make the anchorage uncomfortable; in that case the skipper will hold the boat closer to Cabopino, which is 6 NM back west and far more sheltered. We monitor the forecast 48 hours out and offer free date changes if conditions look unsuitable. The Mangusta 80 includes the jet ski free. For full inclusion details see the fleet page . Festival-day food on board Most groups skip the food trucks ashore and eat on deck. Standard catering is built into the price — light snacks for grazing through the afternoon. For a proper dinner we can add a chef-prepared menu (paella, grilled fish, salads) for €45–65 per person, ordered 72 hours in advance via WhatsApp. The 8-hour Tier A charter at €2,299 leaves comfortable headroom in most budgets for catering, festival tickets and a round of cocktails ashore. Booking timeline and deposits Cala de Mijas Music Festival weekend is the busiest non-July date for our fleet. Tier A boats and the Lagoon 380 sell out 4–6 weeks ahead. To book, message us on WhatsApp with date, group size and boat preference. We confirm within the hour during the working day, hold the slot for 24 hours, and send a deposit link — typically 30% to confirm, balance on the charter day. Cancellation for weather is free up to 48 hours before departure. The main page has the full fleet, current pricing and the WhatsApp button. Two scrolls and you're talking to the skipper. You stay on board for food, drinks and music drifting over the water, then tender ashore for the headline sets. The skipper coordinates timings so you don't miss the acts you care about. TITLE: Marbella Restaurants You Can Arrive at by Boat
DESCRIPTION: Marbella restaurants by boat: 9 beach clubs and chiringuitos with mooring or tender drop, plus 4h charter from €749. Book via WhatsApp today.

Home › Guide › Marbella Restaurants You Can Arrive at by Boat By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers The fastest table in Marbella is the one you reach by sea. Skip the N-340 traffic, the €25 valet and the 20-minute walk from the car park: arrive directly at the chiringuito by tender, or moor stern-to at a Puerto Banús quayside terrace. This guide covers nine restaurants between Estepona and Cabopino that work for a lunch stop on a half-day charter, the practicalities of getting ashore, and how to pair the meal with a swim at Cala del Faro or Río Verde . Puerto Banús quayside: mooring stern-to for lunch Five venues inside the marina take walk-up boat traffic from 12:00 to 16:00. Cipriani (Italian, terrace right on the Levante quay), Astral (Mediterranean fish, opposite the superyacht line), La Sala by the Sea (British-leaning brasserie, easy with kids), Pangea (steaks, lounge atmosphere) and Sumosan (Japanese, end of the harbour). All five sit within a 50m walk of the visitor berths. Our skipper calls Puerto Banús harbour control on VHF 09 about 30 minutes before arrival and you usually have a berth allocated by the time you tie up. Quayside rates for a 2-3 hour lunch run €40-€90 depending on overall length; for boats over 20m we pre-book the day before. None of these venues charge a corkage if you arrive by water — you simply order from their drinks list as usual. Trocadero Arena and the Río Verde anchorage Trocadero Arena sits 1.5NM east of Puerto Banús on the sand at Río Verde. It is the closest serious chiringuito to the marina and the easiest first stop on a 4h Tier A charter at €749. We anchor in 4-5m of water about 100m off the beach, drop the tender, and you step ashore at the lifeguard tower. The kitchen runs a Mediterranean menu: grilled sea bass €32, ibérico croquettes €18, rice dishes from €28 for two. Sunbeds on their stretch of sand cost €40 a pair including a parasol. Service from 13:30; reserve the morning of your charter on +34 952 779 365. From the anchorage you see La Concha and the Golden Mile framing the coast — one of the best lunch views in Marbella. Nikki Beach: the dressy option Nikki Beach Marbella sits 3NM east of Puerto Banús, in front of the Don Carlos hotel. The anchorage is exposed to the east wind, so we check the forecast and only commit if Levante stays under 12 knots. Tender ashore is a 4-minute run. The dress code matters here: no swimwear at the table after 14:00, so guests change on the boat into linen or a dress before the tender shuttle. A two-course lunch with a bottle of rosé runs €70-€110 a head; sunbeds €60 each. Sunday brunches are theatrical and pricey but a good reason to take the 8h charter at €2,299 and stay for the dancing-on-tables 16:00 set. Cabopino chiringuitos: low-key and fish-led Push another 5NM east of Nikki Beach and you reach the dune system at Cabopino. Three chiringuitos work here for a boat lunch: Bono Beach , Andy's Beach and El Oasis . All three are sand-floored, swimwear-OK, with main courses €18-€28 and a kids' menu around €14. Anchorage is sheltered from westerlies and the holding is good in 4m of sand. We drop the hook 120m off Bono Beach's lifeguard post and shuttle in by tender. This is the best stop if you want a quiet lunch without the Puerto Banús showboat energy; it also pairs nicely with a return swim at Cala Cortés on the way back . How the catering-onboard option works If you would rather not go ashore, every charter already includes water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava and light snacks — that is enough for a 2-3 hour trip. For a proper meal onboard we coordinate with you the day before: tapas platters from €18 per person, sushi boxes around €35, paella for the table at €45 per head. We collect the order from Puerto Banús before you board and serve it at anchor in your chosen cove. Drinks remain included on top. This is a good move on August weekends when the popular chiringuitos run two-hour waits for walk-ins. Recommended routes by charter length Charter length Route Lunch stop Price (Tier A) 2h Puerto Banús quay loop Cipriani or Astral, stern-to €749 4h Puerto Banús → Río Verde → return Trocadero Arena, tender ashore €1,499 6h Puerto Banús → Nikki Beach → Cala del Faro swim Nikki Beach (smart-casual) €1,899 8h Puerto Banús → Cabopino → sunset return Bono Beach, swimwear €2,299 All four routes run on Tier A boats (Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39, both 12.5m, up to 9-11 guests). Upgrading to a Mangusta 80 for the day starts at €4,719 for 4h and adds a free jet ski, which makes the swim stop between courses considerably more entertaining. Booking sequence for a lunch charter Pick a route from the table above and message us the date on WhatsApp via the homepage . We hold the boat for 24 hours while you reserve the restaurant directly. Send us the reservation confirmation; we plan timings around the table slot. Pay a 30% deposit to confirm; balance settles on the day in Puerto Banús. Arrive 15 minutes before departure at the Levante quay; the skipper greets you with the drinks already on ice. Frequently asked questions Which Marbella restaurants can I actually arrive at by boat? You can reach Puerto Banús quayside venues (Cipriani, La Sala by the Sea, Astral) directly by mooring stern-to. For beach clubs like Nikki Beach, Trocadero Arena, Bono Beach and the Cabopino chiringuitos, you anchor 100m off the sand and our skipper runs you in by tender. Plan a 1-2NM hop from Puerto Banús — most stops are 10-20 minutes away. Do I need to book a berth in Puerto Banús for lunch? For our fleet under 15m, the skipper handles same-day berthing via VHF 09 on arrival; usually you get a slot within 20 minutes. For yachts over 20m (Mangusta 80, Canados 86, Ferretti 94) we pre-book the day before. Lunch-only stays of 2-3 hours are charged at the short-stay quayside rate, typically €40-€90 depending on length overall. How does a tender drop-off at a beach club work? Your skipper anchors 80-150m off the beach in 4-6m of water, then ferries guests ashore in the tender, two or three at a time. You step off in knee-deep water onto the sand and walk 30 seconds to the chiringuito. Bags, phones and shoes ride in a dry bag. The skipper waits aboard and collects you when you signal by phone or VHF. What is the dress code at Marbella beachfront restaurants? Chiringuitos like Trocadero Arena, Bono Beach and the Cabopino huts are swimwear-friendly at lunch — sarong or shirt over your costume is fine. Puerto Banús quayside venues (Cipriani, Astral) expect smart-casual: linen shirts, dresses, dry shoes. Nikki Beach enforces no swimwear at the table after 14:00, so pack a change of clothes if that is your stop. Can we eat onboard instead of going ashore? Yes — every charter includes water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava and light snacks. For a full meal we can pre-order catering: tapas platters from €18 per person, sushi boxes around €35, or a paella delivered to the boat for €45 per head. Tell us the day before and we collect the order from Puerto Banús before departure. Drinks remain included on top. What is the best route for a half-day lunch charter? From Puerto Banús, head east to Río Verde for a quick swim, anchor off Trocadero Arena (1.5NM, 12 minutes), eat lunch, then continue to Cabopino dunes (another 6NM east) for an afternoon swim before returning. Total distance about 16NM round-trip, comfortably done in a 4h Tier A charter at €749 or in 6h for a more relaxed pace. Are children welcome at these restaurants? Most chiringuitos are family-friendly — Bono Beach and the Cabopino huts have shaded play areas and kids' menus around €12-€16. Trocadero Arena and Nikki Beach accept children at lunchtime but lean adult. Puerto Banús venues like Cipriani are fine for over-eights but not really set up for toddlers. Our boats carry life jackets in every child size from 10kg upwards. Can I combine a boat lunch with a sunset return

TITLE: Cabopino vs Estepona: Best Snorkeling Spots by Boat in Marbella 2041
DESCRIPTION: Cabopino vs Estepona: best snorkeling spots by boat in Marbella 2041. Compare clear coves, depths &amp; nearby boat types. Book your charter today.

Home › Guide › Cabopino vs Estepona: Best Snorkeling Spots by Boat in Marbella 2041 By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers If you’re looking for the best snorkeling spots near Marbella by boat, two names come up again and again: Cabopino and Estepona. Both offer clear Mediterranean water, marine life, and easy anchorage—but they suit different preferences. This guide compares the two, so you can choose the right spot for your 2041 charter from Puerto Banús . Cabopino: Sandy Coves for Beginners Cabopino lies 15 nautical miles west of Puerto Banús, about a 30-40 minute cruise on most powerboats. The seabed here is mostly sand with patches of Posidonia seagrass, keeping water visibility at 10-15m in summer. Depths range from 2-5m, making it safe for children and less confident swimmers. The main snorkeling area is the cove just east of the Cabopino marina breakwater. You’ll find seabream, damselfish, and the occasional octopus hiding under rocks. There are no currents to speak of, and the shoreline is backed by dunes and pine trees—a relaxed vibe. Estepona: Rocky Reefs for Experienced Snorkelers Estepona is 20 NM from Puerto Banús, adding 15-20 minutes each way. The snorkeling here is more dramatic: rocky outcrops, artificial reef structures, and deeper water (5-12m). Visibility often reaches 15-20m, and the marine life is richer—groupers, moray eels, and sometimes rays. The best spot is the Punta Doncella reef, just west of Estepona’s port. You’ll need to be a confident swimmer; there’s no beach exit, so you’ll enter and exit from the boat. We provide buoyancy aids and fins, but it’s not ideal for young children. Boat Options for Your Snorkeling Trip Both destinations are easily reachable from Puerto Banús. For speed, the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) hits 30+ knots and includes a free jet ski—great for exploring coves between dives. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) offers plenty of deck space for sunbathing and a shaded flybridge. Boat Length Pax Cabopino Time Estepona Time Mangusta 80 24m 12 30 min 40 min Azimut 58 Flybridge 17.6m 12 35 min 45 min Azimut 39 12.5m 11 40 min 50 min Astondoa 40 12.5m 9 40 min 50 min What’s Included in Your Charter Every snorkeling charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, and all safety equipment. We also provide snorkel masks, fins, and buoyancy aids. Onboard you’ll find water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks—all included in the price. Spanish IVA (21%) is included too. For the Mangusta 80 (Tier B, from €4,719 for 4 hours), a jet ski is included free. For other boats, pricing is available on request via WhatsApp. Best Time for Snorkeling in Marbella The snorkeling season runs June to September, when water temperatures hit 22-26°C and the sea is calmest. May and October are also good but cooler (18-21°C). Avoid November to March—water drops to 14-16°C, and winds can make the sea choppy. Visibility is best in July and August, often exceeding 15m at both spots. Early morning (9-11am) is ideal: the water is clearest, and there are fewer boats. Which Spot Should You Choose? Pick Cabopino if you’re bringing children, want a relaxed beach-style snorkel, or prefer shallow sandy bottoms. Pick Estepona if you’re an experienced snorkeler, want to see bigger marine life, and don’t mind a longer boat ride. If you have time, do both on a day charter: start at Cabopino, then head west to Estepona for lunch and a second dive. The total round trip from Puerto Banús is about 45 NM—easily doable in 6-8 hours. Frequently asked questions Which snorkeling spot is closer to Puerto Banús? Cabopino is closer, about 15 nautical miles (30-40 minutes by powerboat). Estepona is 20 NM, adding roughly 15 minutes more travel time each way. Do I need my own snorkeling gear? No. Every boat charter includes masks, fins, snorkels, and buoyancy aids. Just bring a towel and sunscreen. Is Cabopino or Estepona better for beginners? Cabopino. The sandy seabed and shallow depths (2-5m) make it safer and more comfortable for less confident swimmers. Estepona’s rocky areas are better for experienced snorkelers. Can I see marine life at both spots? Yes. Cabopino has seabream, damselfish, and occasional octopus. Estepona offers more variety: groupers, moray eels, and sometimes rays near the artificial reef. What is the best time of year for snorkeling in Marbella? June through September. Water temperatures reach 22-26°C, visibility is 10-20m, and the sea is calmest. May and October are also good but cooler (18-21°C). Which boat is best for a snorkeling trip? The Mangusta 80 (24m) is our top pick—fast (30+ kts), stable, and includes a free jet ski. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m) offers great deck space for sunbathing between dives. Do I need to book in advance for a snorkeling charter? Yes. During peak season (July-August), boats book out 2-3 weeks ahead. For 2041, we recommend reserving at least 10 days in advance. Use WhatsApp for availability. Are there any hidden costs for snorkeling charters? No. Our prices include skipper, fuel, insurance, drinks, snacks, and snorkel gear. The only extras are if you want jet ski rental (free with Mangusta 80) or a full catering upgrade. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

TITLE: Snorkeling Spots by Boat: Cabopino vs Estepona in Marbella (2045 Update)
DESCRIPTION: Compare Cabopino vs Estepona snorkeling spots by boat in Marbella. Clear water, marine life &amp; distances. Book your 2025 charter today.

Home › Guide › Snorkeling Spots by Boat: Cabopino vs Estepona in Marbella (2045 Update) By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers If you're looking for the best snorkeling spots by boat Cabopino vs Estepona 2045 , you're in the right place. Both destinations offer clear Mediterranean water, rocky reefs, and plenty of marine life — but they differ in depth, visibility, and accessibility. This guide breaks down the key differences so you can choose the perfect spot for your next charter from Puerto Banús. Why Cabopino and Estepona? Cabopino and Estepona are the two most popular snorkeling destinations within easy reach of Marbella. Both are sheltered by the Sierra Blanca and La Concha mountains, which reduce wind and swell. Cabopino is known for its rocky coves and shallow reefs, while Estepona offers deeper water and clearer visibility. The distance from Puerto Banús is similar — 16 NM to Cabopino and 12 NM to Estepona — making both achievable on a half-day charter. Cabopino Snorkeling: Depth, Marine Life, and Access Cabopino's snorkeling spots are concentrated around the rocky headland near Cala del Faro . The reefs here are 3–6 metres deep, making them ideal for beginners and families. The water temperature in summer reaches 24–26°C, and visibility averages 10–15 metres on calm days. You'll see small fish like bream and damselfish, plus the occasional octopus hiding in crevices. The seabed is a mix of sand and rock, with patches of seagrass. Access is easy: you can anchor close to the rocks and swim straight in. For a comfortable trip, the Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) or Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) are perfect — both are Tier A boats at €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours, including skipper, drinks, and VAT. Estepona Snorkeling: Depth, Marine Life, and Access Estepona's snorkeling is centred on the artificial reef near the port and the natural reefs off Cala Cortés . Depths range from 5 to 12 metres, offering more challenge and variety. Visibility can reach 20 metres when the sea is flat, especially in the morning. The marine life is richer here: you'll find schools of bream, damselfish, and even rays near the sandy patches. The water is slightly cooler (23–25°C) due to deeper currents. Anchoring is possible in sandy areas, but be careful not to damage the seagrass. For a larger group, the Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 pax) is a great choice — it's a Tier B boat at €4,719 for a minimum 4 hours and includes a free jet ski for exploring the coastline. Quick Comparison Table Feature Cabopino Estepona Distance from Puerto Banús 16 NM (30 min) 12 NM (25 min) Depth range 3–6 m 5–12 m Visibility (calm day) 10–15 m 15–20 m Marine life variety Moderate High Best for beginners Yes Intermediate Recommended boat Astondoa 40 / Azimut 39 Mangusta 80 / Azimut 58 Flybridge Price (4h) €1,499 (Tier A) €4,719 (Tier B) Best Time of Year and Day The snorkeling season in Marbella runs from May to October, with peak visibility in July and August. Water temperature hits 26°C in August, making long swims comfortable. The best time of day is between 9:00 and 12:00, when the sea is calmest and the sun is not directly overhead. Afternoon winds from the west can stir up sediment and reduce visibility, especially in Cabopino's shallower coves. Check the wind forecast before booking — a light Levante (east wind) is ideal for both spots. Which Spot Should You Choose? Choose Cabopino if you're a beginner, have young children, or want a relaxed, shallow snorkel in a sheltered cove. The short swim from the boat and calm conditions make it stress-free. Choose Estepona if you're an intermediate snorkeller, want clearer water and more marine life, or don't mind slightly deeper depths. Estepona also has more options for combining snorkeling with a stop for lunch at a beach club. TITLE: Marbella Snorkeling Spots by Boat: Cabopino vs Estepona Compared
DESCRIPTION: Compare Cabopino vs Estepona for the best Marbella snorkeling spots by boat. Clear water, rocky coves, and sea life – find your perfect charter here.

Home › Guide › Marbella Snorkeling Spots by Boat: Cabopino vs Estepona Compared By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers If you're looking for the best Marbella snorkeling spots by boat , the choice usually comes down to two favourites: Cabopino and Estepona. Both are within a short cruise from Puerto Banús, yet they offer completely different underwater experiences. Cabopino is all about sandy, shallow coves and gentle waters – perfect for families and first-timers. Estepona, on the other hand, delivers rocky reefs, clearer water, and a more diverse marine ecosystem. This guide compares both destinations side by side, so you can pick the right spot for your group size, budget, and snorkeling skill level. All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and snorkel gear – no hidden costs. Cabopino: Sandy Shallows and Family-Friendly Snorkeling Cabopino lies about 20 minutes east of Puerto Banús, near the famous Cabopino beach and sand dunes. The seabed here is mostly fine sand with patches of Posidonia seagrass, which attract small fish like seabream, mullet, and the occasional cuttlefish. Water depth rarely exceeds 5 metres within 50 metres of the shore, making it safe for children and weak swimmers. Visibility averages 8–12 metres from May to October. The main snorkeling area is the cove just west of the Cabopino marina entrance. There are no strong currents, and the boat can anchor right next to the snorkeling zone. If your group includes non-snorkelers, they can swim ashore to the beach club or explore the dunes. For a 2-hour charter, the Astondoa 40 (€749/2h, 9 pax) is a popular choice. Estepona: Rocky Reefs and Rich Marine Life Estepona, 30–35 minutes west of Puerto Banús, is the better option for experienced snorkelers. The coastline here is more rugged, with granite boulders and underwater rock formations that create natural habitats for groupers, moray eels, starfish, and even small rays. The clearest spots are Cala del Faro and Cala Cortés, two small coves sheltered from the prevailing westerly winds. Water visibility often exceeds 15 metres in summer. Depths range from 3 metres inshore to 12 metres just 30 metres out. There are no sandy beaches – access is purely by boat. The reward is a much richer biodiversity than Cabopino. Because Estepona is further from Puerto Banús, we recommend a minimum 4-hour charter to allow enough snorkeling time. The Azimut 58 Flybridge (17.6m, 12 pax) is ideal for larger groups who want comfort and speed. Cabopino vs Estepona: Quick Comparison Table Feature Cabopino Estepona Distance from Puerto Banús 20 min 30–35 min Seabed type Sand and seagrass Rocks and boulders Average visibility 8–12 m 12–18 m Water depth (snorkeling area) 1–5 m 3–12 m Best for Families, beginners Experienced snorkelers Marine life diversity Moderate High Beach access Yes (Cabopino beach) No (rocky coast) Recommended charter duration 2–4 hours 4–8 hours Which Boat Should You Book for Snorkeling? The right boat depends on group size and whether you want to combine snorkeling with other activities. For a quick 2-hour snorkel session at Cabopino, the Azimut 39 (11 pax, €749/2h) is the most cost-effective option. If you're heading to Estepona and want to spend the day, the Mangusta 80 (12 pax, from €4,719/4h) includes a free jet ski – perfect for exploring the coves between snorkeling stops. Smaller groups of up to 5 can use the Speedboat (7m, 6 pax) or Dubhe (8m, 5 pax) , both of which are nimble and can anchor close to the rocks. All boats carry snorkel masks, fins, and wetsuits on request. If you're unsure which boat fits your plans, contact us on WhatsApp and we'll match you to the right vessel. Best Time of Year for Snorkeling in Marbella Snorkeling conditions vary across the year. The peak season is June to September, when water temperatures hit 22–26°C and visibility is at its best (12–18 metres). May and October are still good, with cooler water (18–21°C) but fewer crowds. From November to March, the water drops to 15–17°C and visibility can reduce to 5–8 metres due to plankton blooms and winter swells. That said, sunny winter days can still offer decent snorkeling at Cabopino if you wear a wetsuit (we provide them). Estepona tends to have clearer water in winter because its rocky seabed doesn't stir up sediment as easily. For the best experience, book a morning charter – the sea is calmest between 9:00 and 12:00, and the sun angle gives the best underwater light. How to Combine Snorkeling with a Full Day on the Water Many guests treat snorkeling as just one part of a longer itinerary. A typical 8-hour charter from Puerto Banús might include: a morning snorkel at Estepona's Cala del Faro, a late lunch at a chiringuito in Cabopino, an afternoon swim stop near the Golden Mile , and a sunset cruise back past the Marbella marina. The Ferretti 94 (29m, 12 pax) is spacious enough for a full-day trip with a proper dining area and sun pads. If you're on a tighter budget, the Bandido (11.5m, 8 pax) offers a more intimate experience. Whichever boat you choose, your skipper will know the best anchor spots and can adjust the plan based on weather and sea conditions. Just let us know your preferences when you book. Frequently Asked Questions Which is better for beginners: Cabopino or Estepona? Cabopino is better for beginners. Its shallow, sandy seabed and calm waters make it safe and easy to snorkel. Estepona has more rocks and deeper drop-offs, which can be challenging for novices. How long does it take to reach Cabopino from Puerto Banús? Cabopino is about 20–25 minutes by powerboat from Puerto Banús. Estepona is slightly further, around 30–35 minutes. Both are easily covered in a 2-hour or 4-hour charter. Do you provide snorkeling equipment? Yes, we provide masks, snorkels, and fins on every charter at no extra cost. Just let us know your sizes when booking, and we'll have them ready on board. What marine life can I expect to see? In Cabopino, expect small fish, sea bream, and occasional octopus in the seagrass. Estepona offers more variety: groupers, moray eels, starfish, and sometimes rays near the rocky reefs. Which boat is best for a snorkeling trip? For small groups (5 pax), the Speedboat or Dubhe are ideal. For up to 11 pax, the Azimut 39 (€749/2h) offers great value. Larger groups should book the Mangusta 80 (12 pax, from €4,719/4h). Can I combine snorkeling with other activities? Absolutely. Many guests combine snorkeling with lunch at a beach club, a stop at Cabopino beach, or a jet ski session (included on the Mangusta 80). Your skipper can tailor the itinerary. Is the water cold in Marbella? Sea temperatures range from 15°C in winter to 26°C in August. The best snorkeling months are June to September when visibility exceeds 15 metres. We provide wetsuits on request for cooler months. Do I need to book in advance? Yes, especially during summer (July–August). TITLE: Best Photoshoot Locations on a Marbella Sunset Cruise
DESCRIPTION: Discover the best photoshoot locations on a Marbella sunset cruise. From Puerto Banús to Cala Cortés, find golden-hour spots for stunning photos. Book now.

Home › Guide › Best Photoshoot Locations on a Marbella Sunset Cruise By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella's coastline is a photographer's dream during golden hour, and a sunset cruise offers access to backdrops you simply can't reach from land. Whether you're planning a couple's shoot, a family portrait session, or influencer content, the light between 19:30 and 21:30 (June–August) transforms the Mediterranean into a palette of amber, rose, and violet. This guide covers the best photoshoot locations on a Marbella sunset cruise, from the glitz of Puerto Banús to the secluded coves near Cabopino. Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and insurance — so you can focus on the shot. Puerto Banús Skyline and Marina Departing from Puerto Banús means you start your shoot with the most recognisable skyline on the Costa del Sol. The marina's superyachts, palm-lined promenade, and the silhouette of La Concha mountain create a layered composition. Position your subjects on the bow of a Marbella sunset cruise boat, with the marina receding behind them. The golden light catches the white hulls and glass balustrades of the port's architecture. Best time: 20 minutes before sunset, when the sun is low over the western end of the marina. Cala del Faro – Cliffside Drama Ten minutes west of Puerto Banús, Cala del Faro offers a rugged contrast to the marina's polish. The lighthouse at Cabo de Plata stands on a rocky headland, and the cliffs here catch the last warm rays before the sun dips. For a dramatic portrait, frame your subject with the lighthouse in the background and the sea catching the orange light. The water here is deeper and clearer, so reflections are sharp. Boats like the Astondoa 40 can anchor close enough for safe boarding without disturbing the scene. Cala Cortés – Turquoise Water and White Sand This small cove between Marbella and Estepona is a hidden gem. The sand is fine and pale, and the water takes on a brilliant turquoise in late afternoon light. It's ideal for beach-style shots without the crowds. The boat can anchor 20 metres offshore, and the shallow depth (1–2 metres) means you can wade in for waist-deep water portraits. The Mangusta 80 (24m) has a swim platform that makes boarding easy. The cove faces southwest, so the sun sets directly behind the beach — perfect for silhouetted couple shots. Estepona Coastline – Pastel-Coloured Backdrop As you cruise west, the Estepona shoreline reveals rows of whitewashed buildings with terracotta roofs and the occasional pastel facade. The light here is softer than in Marbella, with less haze. Photograph the buildings from the water, using the boat's railing as a foreground element. The Azimut 39 has a low-profile windshield that won't obstruct the view. Best results come from a telephoto lens (70–200mm) to compress the distance and isolate the colourful architecture against the warm sky. Cabopino Dunes and Nature Reserve East of Marbella, the Cabopino dunes offer a wilder backdrop. The protected sand dunes and pine forest contrast with the manicured coastline. From the boat, you can capture the dunes in the foreground with the sea beyond. This area is less built-up, so the horizon is clean. The light here is particularly good in early autumn, when the sun sets further south. Use a wide-angle lens (16–35mm) to include both the dunes and the expansive sky. Boats like the Mangusta 80 can cruise slowly along this stretch, giving you time to compose multiple shots. Sotogrande and the Guadiaro River Mouth Further east, the Guadiaro River mouth creates a unique estuary landscape. The sandbanks and shallow channels reflect the sunset colours in a way that open sea cannot. This spot works well for abstract, artistic shots — the patterns in the sand and water create natural leading lines. The best time is 30 minutes after sunset, when the sky deepens to indigo and the water turns silver. A longer exposure (1–2 seconds) will blur the water's movement for a dreamy effect. All our Marbella sunset cruise boats can access this area, though the larger vessels may need to anchor further out. Practical Tips for Your Photoshoot Cruise Book the right duration: A 2-hour charter (€749 for Tier A boats) is enough for one main location. For multiple coves, choose 4 or 6 hours. Bring a polarising filter: Reduces glare on the water and deepens the sky colours. Check the wind: Light wind (under 10 knots) keeps the water calm for clear reflections. The skipper will advise. Use the swim platform: The Azimut 58 Flybridge has a large platform that doubles as a shooting stage at water level. Communicate with the skipper: They know the best anchor points for each cove and can adjust the boat's position. Frequently asked questions What time is best for a sunset photoshoot cruise in Marbella? Golden hour typically starts around 19:30 in spring and autumn, and 20:00 in summer. For the best light, book a 2-hour charter departing at 18:30 or 19:00, giving you 30–45 minutes of soft, warm light before the sun dips below the horizon. Which boat is best for a photoshoot on a Marbella sunset cruise? The Mangusta 80 (24m) offers a wide flybridge and spacious bow, ideal for group shots. For smaller groups, the Azimut 39 (12.5m, 11 pax) has a sleek, modern profile that photographs well. Both depart from Puerto Banús. Can I bring my own photographer on the sunset cruise? Yes, you can bring a professional photographer or a friend with a camera. There is no extra charge for additional passengers as long as the total does not exceed the boat's capacity. Just let us know when booking. What are the best spots for photos along the Marbella coast? Top spots include Puerto Banús marina (luxury yachts and La Concha mountain), Cala del Faro (cliffs and lighthouse), Cala Cortés (turquoise water and sand), and the Estepona coastline (colourful buildings). Each offers unique backdrops during golden hour. What is included in the sunset cruise charter price? TITLE: Age Policy for Kids &amp; Teens on Marbella Yacht Charters: What to Know
DESCRIPTION: Learn the marbella yacht charter age policy for kids and teens. Minimum ages, safety rules, and family-friendly boats departing Puerto Banús. Book now!

Home › Guide › Age Policy for Kids & Teens on Marbella Yacht Charters: What to Know By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Planning a family yacht charter in Marbella? Understanding the marbella yacht charter age policy for kids and teens ensures a safe and enjoyable day on the water. From minimum ages to safety gear and recommended boats, here’s everything you need to know before setting sail from Puerto Banús. Minimum Age Requirements for Children Most charters accept infants from 12 months old . For safety, children under 12 must wear a life jacket whenever on deck. Always confirm your child’s age when booking, as some boats may have stricter policies. The skipper will provide a safety briefing for the whole family. Life Jackets and Safety Gear Life jackets in child and toddler sizes are supplied free on every charter. Our fleet includes boats like the Astondoa 40 and Lagoon 380, both with ample deck space for kids to move safely. The skipper will also demonstrate emergency procedures and point out safety equipment locations. Teens and Jet Ski Use Teens aged 12 and over can drive a jet ski on Tier B boats (e.g., Mangusta 80) but only with adult supervision. The jet ski is included free on 4-hour+ charters. For younger teens, consider a slower speedboat like the Speedboat (7m, 6 pax) for a more controlled experience. Best Boats for Families with Kids Choose stable, spacious boats for young children. The Lagoon 380 (catamaran, 10 pax) offers minimal rocking and shaded areas. The Astondoa 40 (9 pax) has a large aft deck, perfect for toddlers. For larger families, the Azimut 58 Flybridge (12 pax) provides plenty of room and a flybridge for teens to enjoy the view. Swimming and Water Activities All ages are welcome to swim at stops like Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés. Children under 12 must wear a life jacket in the water unless held by an adult. The skipper will choose calm, shallow spots ideal for family dips. Bring your own snorkel gear for the kids — our charter packages include soft drinks and snacks to keep everyone energised. Booking and Passenger Limits Children count towards the total passenger capacity. For example, the Astondoa 40 holds 9 pax total, so a family of 4 adults and 5 kids fits perfectly. The Azimut 39 (11 pax) offers more flexibility. Always mention children’s ages when booking to ensure the right boat and safety equipment. What’s Included for Families Every charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, insurance, life jackets, and a safety briefing. Drinks (water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava) and light snacks are provided. You can bring your own baby food or special snacks — no problem. The skipper will also help with any specific needs, like finding a quiet spot for nap time. Frequently asked questions What is the minimum age for kids on a Marbella yacht charter? Most charters accept infants from 12 months old. For safety, children under 12 must wear a life jacket on deck. Always confirm with the operator when booking, as some boats may have different policies for very young children. Can teenagers drive a jet ski during the charter? Yes, teenagers aged 12 and over can drive a jet ski on Tier B boats like the Mangusta 80, but only under direct adult supervision. The jet ski is included free for 4-hour+ charters on these boats. Are life jackets provided for children? Yes, life jackets in various sizes are provided free of charge for all children. We recommend bringing a favourite floatie or swim vest for added comfort, but our safety equipment covers all ages. Which boats are best for families with young kids? Boats with spacious decks and stable hulls are ideal. The Lagoon 380 (catamaran, 10 pax) offers excellent stability and shade. The Astondoa 40 (9 pax) has a large cockpit area, perfect for toddlers. Is there a minimum age for booking a private charter? No, there is no minimum age for the charter itself. All ages are welcome, but the responsible adult must be at least 18 years old to sign the contract. Children are counted in the total passenger capacity. What safety measures are in place for kids on board? All charters include a licensed skipper trained in safety protocols, plus life jackets, first aid kits, and safety briefing before departure. Málaga Airport to Puerto Banús: Distance & Time Málaga-Costa del Sol Airport (AGP) is the main gateway for the Costa del Sol. From the arrivals hall to the marina at Puerto Banús, the distance is exactly 45 km. By car, the journey takes 35–50 minutes depending on traffic. The route follows the A-7 motorway along the coast, passing through Torremolinos, Benalmádena, and Fuengirola. Peak season (June–September) can add 10–15 minutes, especially on Friday afternoons and Sunday evenings. Transfer Options: Cost & Convenience Private taxi: €65–€90 one-way. Pre-book via Welcome Pickups or local companies. Door-to-door, no waiting. Shared shuttle: €15–€25 per person. Companies like ALSA or Suntransfers. 45–60 min journey. Good for solo travellers. Rental car: From €30/day with Avis or Hertz at AGP. Parking at Puerto Banús costs €20–€30/day. Useful if you plan to explore Marbella after the charter. Train + bus: Not recommended. Cercanías train goes to Fuengirola (€6), then a bus to Marbella (€3). Total 1.5–2 hours. For large suitcases, we recommend leaving them at your hotel or using a left-luggage service near the port. Puerto Banús has several luggage storage points, including Locker Marbella on Calle Ribera, about 5 minutes from our dock. Alternatively, ask your taxi driver to drop your bags at your accommodation before heading to the marina. Local Tips for a Seamless Day Book your taxi 24–48 hours in advance during high season (July–August). Arrive at the marina 15 minutes before departure to meet your skipper and stow belongings. If you want to stock up on extra snacks or drinks, ask your driver for a short stop at a Mercadona or Carrefour . After the charter, explore the Golden Mile or dine at one of the chiringuitos on the beach — many are a short walk from Puerto Banús. In Marbella, the atmosphere is electric – but the beaches and restaurants get packed. A private yacht charter lets you enjoy the holiday without the crowds. Home › Guide › Astondoa 40 vs Azimut 39: Fuel Cost Comparison for Marbella Charters By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers When booking a yacht charter in Marbella, two of the most popular choices in our Tier A fleet are the Astondoa 40 and the Azimut 39 . All charters include fuel, skipper, drinks, snacks, and IVA—but understanding the numbers helps you plan smarter. Astondoa 40 vs Azimut 39: Engine Specs and Fuel Consumption Both boats are powered by twin diesel engines, but with different configurations that affect fuel burn. Astondoa 40: 2× Volvo Penta D6-370 (740 hp total). Cruise speed ~22 knots, fuel consumption ~110 L/h. Azimut 39: 2× Cummins QSB5.9-380 (760 hp total). Cruise speed ~22 knots, fuel consumption ~120 L/h. The Astondoa 40 is about 8% more fuel-efficient at cruising speed, burning roughly 10 fewer litres per hour. Over a full 8-hour day, that difference adds up to 80 litres saved. Real Fuel Cost Comparison for 8-Hour Charter Based on current Marbella diesel prices of approximately €1.90 per litre (as of mid-2025), here are the estimated fuel costs for an 8-hour charter: Boat Fuel consumption (L/h) Total fuel (8h) Fuel cost (€) Astondoa 40 110 880 L €1,672 Azimut 39 120 960 L €1,824 The Azimut 39 costs roughly €152 more in fuel for an 8-hour day. However, both boats are priced at the same Tier A charter rate : €2,299 for 8 hours, which already includes all fuel. So the fuel cost difference doesn't affect your out-of-pocket expense—it's more about the boat's efficiency profile. Half-Day Charter (4 Hours) Fuel Savings For a 4-hour charter (€1,149), the fuel burn is halved: Astondoa 40: ~440 L → €836 Azimut 39: ~480 L → €912 The Astondoa 40 saves about €76 on fuel over the Azimut 39 for a half-day. While still within the all-inclusive rate, the Astondoa is the more economical choice if you're tracking environmental impact or just want the lighter fuel footprint. Which Boat Should You Choose? Fuel cost is only one factor. Consider these points: Passenger capacity: Astondoa 40 (9 pax) vs Azimut 39 (11 pax). The Azimut fits two extra guests. Onboard layout: The Astondoa 40 has a spacious aft deck and sun pads; the Azimut 39 offers a more enclosed saloon with larger windows. Charter value: Both are identical in price, so if you need more seats, the Azimut 39 is better value per person. For smaller groups or those prioritising fuel efficiency, the Astondoa 40 is the winner. For larger parties, the Azimut 39 gives you more space without extra fuel cost to you. Where You'll Cruise from Puerto Banús Both boats depart from Puerto Banús , the heart of Marbella's luxury marina. Typical itineraries include cruising past the Golden Mile , stopping at Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés for swimming, and heading toward Cabopino or Estepona for lunch. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter in August: Peak Season Guide
DESCRIPTION: Marbella yacht charter in August: peak-season prices from €749/2h, 30°C sea, book 14+ days ahead. WhatsApp us to lock your slot.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter in August: Peak Season Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers August is the busiest charter month in Marbella, with sea temperatures peaking at 23-25°C, near-zero rain risk and Puerto Banús quay packed by mid-morning. Our fleet runs at 85-95% capacity through the month, so this guide covers what to expect on price, weather, availability and which slots disappear first. If you want the short version: book mid-week if you can, lock weekends in 14+ days ahead, and start with the main Boat Rental Marbella hub for the full fleet overview. August pricing — what stays fixed and what doesn't Our headline pricing does not surge in August. The Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 stay at €749 for 2 hours and €2,299 for 8 hours, all-inclusive of skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance and 21% IVA. The Mangusta 80 (both white and grey variants) remains €4,719 for the 4-hour minimum with a jet ski included free. Pricing for other boats — Pershing 46, Fairline Targa 12m, Ferretti 94, Canados 86, Maiora 26m and the rest — stays consistent year-round and is shared on WhatsApp request. What does change in August is the mix of inventory available. Discounted last-minute windows that appear in May or October do not exist in August: every slot books at standard rates. The cheapest entry-level slot — Tier A 2-hour mornings — disappears first. See the full fleet and pricing on the homepage for comparisons. Sea, weather and wind in August August is Marbella's warmest month. Air temperatures sit at 29-32°C inland and 26-29°C on the water, with sea temperatures averaging 23-25°C and peaking around 26°C in the third week. Rain risk is under 2% on any given day. The dominant pattern is glassy water from 09:00 to roughly 13:00, then thermal winds pick up. Morning (09:00-13:00): winds under 5 knots, flat water, best for paddleboarding and first-time guests. Afternoon (14:00-18:00): Poniente (west) or Levante (east) winds at 12-18 knots, occasionally 20+. Evening (18:00-21:30): winds drop, sea calms, sunset 21:15-21:45 early August. Skippers adapt routing daily. On Levante days the fleet runs west toward Estepona and Sotogrande for shelter; on Poniente days it heads east toward Cabopino and Cala del Faro. Cancellations for weather are under 3% across the month. Which boats book out first The booking order across August is predictable. Entry-tier boats go first because of the €749/2h price point, then the headline Mangustas, then the mid-size sport boats. Boat Capacity Typical lead time (weekends) Astondoa 40 / Azimut 39 9-11 pax 14-21 days Mangusta 80 (white & grey) 12 pax 10-14 days Lagoon 380 catamaran 10 pax 7-10 days Pershing 46, Fairline Targa 12m 10 pax 5-10 days Ferretti 94, Canados 86, Maiora 26m 12 pax 3-7 days For the full list, the Mariah SX21, Speedboat and Dubhe are good fallback options for smaller groups — they have higher mid-August availability than the flagship boats. Peak dates within August Three weekends are functionally booked solid 10+ days out: 8-9 August, 15-16 August (Asunción bank holiday weekend), and 22-23 August. The 15 August public holiday in particular pulls in domestic Spanish demand on top of international visitors. If you can shift to a Tuesday, Wednesday or Thursday, availability is 20-30% easier and you get the same boats and pricing. The 18:00-20:00 sunset window is the second most popular, especially for couples and small groups. The 13:00-16:00 midday slot is the easiest to book but the hottest — pick a boat with proper shade if you go midday (Mangusta 80, Canados 86, Azimut 58 Flybridge). Sunset is the best time slot in August — temperatures drop to a comfortable 25-27°C, winds calm down after 19:30, and La Concha mountain glows orange. Sunset sits at around 21:15-21:45 in early-to-mid August. The 18:00-20:00 evening slot is our most-booked window after weekend mornings. Cava and snacks included, as on every charter.
