The following table outlines the refund percentage of the total charter cost: Cancellation Notice Refund Percentage Deposit Treatment 30+ days before departure 100% refund of total cost Deposit fully refunded 14–29 days before departure 50% refund of total cost Deposit retained, 50% balance due Less than 14 days before departure No refund Full cost charged No-show No refund Full cost charged This applies to all boats in our fleet, from the Astondoa 40 (€749/2h) to the Mangusta 80 (€4,719 minimum 4h). Rescheduling Your Charter If your plans shift, rescheduling is often better than cancelling. Notify us at least 7 days before departure, and we will move your booking to another available date within the same season (typically May to October). No additional fees are charged for rescheduling. This applies to all boats, including the Azimut 39 and Ferretti 94 . Last-minute rescheduling (less than 7 days) is at our discretion and may incur a fee. Weather and Safety Cancellations Marbella enjoys over 300 sunny days a year, but the Mediterranean can turn rough. If the captain or operator cancels the charter due to unsafe weather (e.g., high winds, storms), you receive a full refund or can reschedule at no cost. Personal disappointment with cloud cover or light drizzle does not qualify. The captain’s decision is final for safety. Check our fleet page for boat-specific weather limits. Group Cancellations and No-Shows The policy applies per boat, not per person. If one guest cancels, the group remains responsible for the full charter cost. We recommend travel insurance that covers individual cancellations. No-shows (failure to arrive at Puerto Banús at the departure time) result in full forfeiture of the charter cost with no rescheduling option. Arrive at least 15 minutes early to avoid this. How to Cancel or Reschedule To cancel or reschedule, send a WhatsApp message to the number on your booking confirmation. Include your booking reference and reason. We will confirm receipt and process the refund or new date within 24 hours. Refunds are returned to the original payment method within 5–10 business days. For urgent changes, WhatsApp is fastest—email may take longer. Exceptional Circumstances We understand life happens. Exceptional circumstances, such as a medical emergency with a doctor’s note, are reviewed case-by-case. We may offer a partial refund or reschedule even outside the standard timeline. Travel insurance remains the best protection for unexpected events. Contact us via WhatsApp to discuss your situation. For cancellations between 14 and 29 days, the deposit is retained and you owe 50% of the remaining balance. Can I get a refund if someone in my group cancels? No. The policy applies to the entire boat charter, not per person. If your group size reduces, you remain responsible for the full charter cost. Consider travel insurance for individual changes. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter Credit Card Fees Explained
DESCRIPTION: Avoid surprise charges on your Marbella yacht charter. We explain credit card fees, deposit policies, and how to pay with zero surcharge. Book now.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter Credit Card Fees Explained By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers When booking a Marbella yacht charter , understanding how credit card fees work can save you money. Many operators add a surcharge for card payments, and we’re transparent about ours. This guide breaks down exactly what you’ll pay, how to avoid extra costs, and what’s included in every charter from Boat Rental Marbella . Our Credit Card Surcharge Policy We accept Visa, Mastercard, and American Express for all bookings. A 2.5% surcharge applies to Visa and Mastercard transactions, and 3.5% for Amex. This covers the processing fees charged by our payment gateway. To avoid this, choose bank transfer (SEPA or international) or pay in cash on arrival – both with zero surcharge. How to Pay Without Extra Fees The simplest way to avoid the surcharge is a bank transfer. We provide our IBAN upon booking confirmation. Alternatively, bring euros in cash to the marina on the day. Cash payments are accepted for deposits and balances. For large amounts (e.g., a €4,719 Mangusta 80 charter), bank transfer is more practical. Deposit and Payment Schedule To secure your Marbella yacht charter , a 30% deposit is required. The remaining 70% is due 7 days before departure. If you pay the deposit by card, the surcharge applies only to that portion. The balance can be paid via bank transfer or cash to avoid further fees. All prices include 21% Spanish IVA, so no surprises at checkout. What’s Included in Every Charter Our prices are all-inclusive: a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and VAT. For example, an Astondoa 40 costs from €749 for 2 hours to €2,299 for 8 hours – no hidden charges. The Mangusta 80 (Tier B) starts at €4,719 for 4 hours, which includes a free jet ski. Why We Charge a Card Fee Payment processors take a cut of each transaction – typically 1.5–3% for cards. Rather than absorbing this cost into our base prices (which would raise them for everyone), we pass it on only to card users. This keeps our cash and bank-transfer prices lower. It’s a common practice among yacht charter operators in Marbella and across the Costa del Sol. Comparing Payment Methods Method Surcharge Best for Bank transfer (SEPA) 0% Deposits & balances Cash (euros) 0% Balance on day Visa/Mastercard 2.5% Quick deposits American Express 3.5% Large amounts Frequently Asked Questions Do you charge a credit card fee on Marbella yacht charters? Yes, a 2.5% surcharge applies for Visa and Mastercard payments, and 3.5% for American Express. We recommend bank transfer or cash to avoid this fee entirely. How can I pay without a credit card surcharge? Pay by bank transfer (SEPA or international) or cash in euros on arrival. Both methods incur zero additional fees. What deposit is required to book a yacht in Marbella? A 30% deposit is needed to confirm your reservation. The remaining 70% is due 7 days before departure. Deposits are fully refundable if cancelled 14+ days prior. Are there any hidden fees in your pricing? No. All our prices include the licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and 21% Spanish IVA. What you see is what you pay, minus any optional extras. Can I pay with a debit card? Yes, debit cards are accepted and treated like credit cards – the same 2.5% surcharge applies for Visa/Mastercard debit. Bank transfer avoids this. What happens if I cancel my booking? Cancel 14 or more days before your charter for a full refund. Within 7-13 days, you lose 50% of the deposit. Less than 7 days, no refund. Do you accept PayPal or other digital wallets? Currently we only accept bank transfer, cash, and credit/debit cards via secure online link. PayPal is not available. Is the credit card fee refundable if I cancel? No, card processing fees are non-refundable and charged by the payment gateway. If you cancel, we refund the charter amount minus the card fee. Understanding Marbella yacht charter credit card fees helps you budget accurately. For a stress-free booking, opt for bank transfer or cash. Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter: Cash, Cards & Currency Tips By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Whether you're chartering an Astondoa 40 for a sunset cruise or a Mangusta 80 for a full day on the Mediterranean, knowing how to handle money in Marbella makes the experience smoother. This guide covers cash vs card, where to exchange currency, and how to avoid fees so you can focus on the sea. Cash or card for your Marbella yacht charter? Both have their place. For the charter fee itself, card is standard — we accept Visa, Mastercard, and bank transfers. For onboard tips, dock fees, and spontaneous purchases from beach vendors, cash is more practical. ATMs in Puerto Banús are plentiful but charge €3–5 per withdrawal, so take out larger amounts less often. Currency exchange rates in Puerto Banús Exchange offices in the marina offer convenience but at a cost — typically 3–5% worse than the interbank rate. A better strategy: withdraw Euros from a bank ATM using a fee-free travel card, or order currency from your home bank before departure. Avoid airport exchange desks entirely. What's included in our charter prices All our charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish IVA (21%). No hidden fees. For example, our Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) cost €749 for 2 hours or €2,299 for 8 hours — all inclusive. The Mangusta 80 starts at €4,719 for 4 hours and includes a free jet ski. Tipping the crew: cash is king If you're happy with the service, a 10–15% tip is customary. Cash ensures the crew receives the full amount. Some guests prefer to tip at the end of the day in an envelope — simple and appreciated. Foreign transaction fees on cards When paying by card, your bank may charge a foreign transaction fee of 1–3%. Check before you travel. Some travel credit cards have zero foreign fees — those are ideal for your Marbella yacht charter . For the charter deposit, we recommend a card with good fraud protection. Budgeting for extras Beyond the charter fee, budget for: Tips: 10–15% of charter cost Dock fees: €20–50 if you stop at a marina Water toys: jet skis, paddleboards (some included on Mangusta 80) Food upgrades: private chef or premium catering (ask us) Most extras are best paid in cash on the day. Best places to get Euros in Marbella For the best rates, use a bank ATM (not a Euronet or independent machine). Banks like Santander, BBVA, and CaixaBank have branches in Marbella and Puerto Banús. If you must use an exchange office, compare rates at two or three before committing. Frequently asked questions Can I pay for a Marbella yacht charter with a credit card? Yes, we accept major credit cards for deposits and final payments. However, some smaller operators may prefer bank transfer or cash. Always confirm before booking to avoid surprises. Should I bring cash or card for my yacht charter in Marbella? Both are useful. Card is best for the charter fee and larger expenses. Cash is handy for tips, dock fees, and buying from beach vendors. ATMs in Puerto Banús are convenient but charge 3–5€ per withdrawal. What currency is used in Marbella? The Euro (EUR) is the official currency. Most businesses accept cards, but cash is still king at smaller bars, market stalls, and for tipping the crew. Are there currency exchange offices in Puerto Banús? Yes, several exchange offices operate in Puerto Banús, but their rates are typically 3–5% worse than interbank rates. Use a bank ATM or a fee-free travel card for better value. Do I need to tip the yacht crew in cash? Cash tips are appreciated and ensure the crew receives the full amount. 10–15% of the charter fee is standard if you're happy with the service. Cards can be used but cash is simpler. What is the best way to get Euros for my yacht charter? Order Euros from your home bank before travel or use a fee-free ATM in Marbella. Avoid exchange desks at airports and marinas, as their rates are poor. Are there any hidden fees when paying for a yacht charter? Our prices include VAT (21%), skipper, fuel, drinks, and snacks. No hidden fees. If you pay with a foreign card, your bank may charge a foreign transaction fee (typically 1–3%). Can I pay in US Dollars or British Pounds for a charter? No, all charters are priced in Euros. Some operators may accept other currencies at a poor exchange rate. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter Deposit Rules: What to Expect
DESCRIPTION: Marbella yacht charter deposit rules explained: 30% to confirm, balance 7 days before, refunds, and 1 simple WhatsApp booking flow.

Home › Guide › Marbella Yacht Charter Deposit Rules: What to Expect By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Booking a yacht in Marbella should not feel like signing a mortgage. Our deposit rules are deliberately simple: 30% to confirm the date, 70% seven days before departure, and a full refund if the captain calls it off for weather . Everything below explains the small print in plain English so you can budget honestly before you message us on WhatsApp — and so there are no surprises when the balance reminder lands in your inbox. If you are still comparing options, our main charter guide covers the fleet and routes first. The 30% booking deposit, explained The booking deposit on every Marbella yacht charter we run is 30% of the total charter price . It is paid the moment you confirm the date and boat — usually within an hour of the WhatsApp conversation — and it does one thing: it locks the slot. Until the deposit lands, the date stays open to other guests, which matters in July and August when Saturdays book three weeks out. The deposit is taken by SEPA bank transfer, card link, Revolut or Wise. Cash is only accepted for in-person last-minute walk-ups at Puerto Banús. On a Tier A boat like the Astondoa 40 at €749 for two hours, that is €224.70 to confirm. On a Tier B booking like the Mangusta 80 at €4,719 for four hours, the deposit is €1,415.70. Every figure on our site is already inclusive of Spanish IVA at 21%, so the percentage applies to the headline price, not a net figure plus tax. When the balance is due The remaining 70% is due seven calendar days before departure . We send a single reminder with the same payment link or IBAN you used for the deposit, and most guests settle it within a day. If you book inside that seven-day window — common for last-minute August trips or weekend changes of plan — the full amount is paid in one go on confirmation, usually within 24 hours. Why seven days? It is the cut-off where we commit fuel, provisioning, skipper rota and dock fees for your specific date. Paying late genuinely risks the slot during peak weekends, because the waiting list for boats like the Azimut 39 and Pershing 46 fills the same morning a date frees up. Is there a separate damage deposit? For Tier A boats — Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 — there is no extra damage deposit . Comprehensive hull and third-party insurance is already included in the price you see, alongside fuel, the licensed skipper, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava and light snacks. You pay the headline number and nothing else. On larger yachts — the Mangusta 80, Ferretti 94, Canados 86, Maiora 26m and Azimut 58 Flybridge — we may ask for a refundable security hold of €1,000 to €3,000 against a card. It is a pre-authorisation, not a charge: the funds are ring-fenced on your card but never actually leave your account unless there is a documented incident. The hold is released within 48 hours of return once the boat is checked in. Deposit amounts by boat tier Tier Example boats Charter price 30% deposit Security hold A — 2h slot Astondoa 40, Azimut 39 €749 €224.70 None A — 8h day Astondoa 40, Azimut 39 €2,299 €689.70 None B — 4h minimum Mangusta 80 (white/grey) €4,719 €1,415.70 €1,000-€2,000 Premium 24m+ Ferretti 94, Canados 86, Maiora 26m On request 30% of quote €2,000-€3,000 The Tier B Mangusta 80 price includes a free jet ski tow, which is unusual at this price point on the Costa del Sol. For premium 24m+ bookings, message us via the contact page for a fixed quote — we do not list hourly rates we cannot honour. Cancellation and refund timeline The refund window mirrors the way slots resell. The earlier you cancel, the easier it is for us to fill the date, so the more we can give back: 14+ days before departure: 100% of the deposit refunded. 7-13 days before: 50% of the deposit refunded. Inside 7 days: deposit is non-refundable — the boat is off the market. Weather cancellation by the captain: 100% refund or free reschedule. The weather rule matters. If forecast winds top 20 knots or the sea state hits 4+, our captain calls the trip — not you. You can take a full refund within five working days or move the booking to any open date in the next twelve months. Detail on how we read the Levante and Poniente winds is in our Marbella weather guide. Transfers, changes and group splits Plans change. If you need to move your date and give us seven or more days notice, we transfer 100% of the deposit to a new booking within twelve months, subject to availability and any price difference for peak dates — an April midweek deposit moving to an August Saturday will need topping up to the peak rate. Same-week transfers are case-by-case and depend on whether we can resell the original slot. For group bookings where one guest drops out, the deposit stays with the lead booker. We do not split deposits across multiple cards on a single charter — collect from your group in advance, then one person pays us. It saves chasing IBANs at 11pm the night before. Payment methods and invoicing We accept SEPA bank transfer in euros (instant or standard), card via Stripe link, Revolut, Wise and cash in person at Puerto Banús. We do not take cryptocurrency or PayPal — the fees and chargeback risk do not work for this price point. Every payment generates a Spanish invoice with IVA broken out at 21% , emailed within 24 hours. Companies booking for client entertainment can request the invoice in their CIF, which is useful for Spanish VAT recovery. If you are flying in and want the deposit on a corporate card but the balance on a personal one, just tell us — we can switch the link between payments. Full breakdown of what is and is not included is in the inclusions guide. Booking flow on WhatsApp, step by step From first message to confirmed slot is usually under an hour: Message us with date, group size and rough budget. We reply with two or three suitable boats and a fixed all-in price. You pick the boat — we send a payment link for the 30% deposit. Deposit lands, slot is locked, you receive a written confirmation with the meeting point at Puerto Banús. Seven days before, we send the balance reminder. On the day, you meet the skipper at the dock, no further paperwork. That is the whole flow. No PDF contracts, no broker chains, no scope creep on the price. If anything in the deposit rules is unclear before you book, ask first on WhatsApp — we would rather answer a question now than refund a misunderstood booking later. For a fuller picture of routes and timing, the half-day routes piece shows where the deposit actually takes you. Frequently asked questions How much is the booking deposit for a Marbella yacht charter? Our booking deposit is 30% of the total charter price, paid by bank transfer or card link once you confirm the date and boat on WhatsApp. The deposit secures the slot — without it, the date stays open to other guests. The remaining 70% is due 7 days before departure. For last-minute bookings inside 7 days, the full amount is paid in one go, usually within 24 hours of agreeing the booking. Is there a separate damage or security deposit on top of the price? On Tier A boats (Astondoa 40, Azimut 39) there is no extra damage deposit — comprehensive insurance and Spanish IVA are already baked into the €749-€2,299 price. On larger yachts like the Mangusta 80, Ferretti 94 or Canados 86, we may ask for a refundable security hold of €1,000-€3,000 against a card, released within 48 hours of return if the boat comes back in the same condition it left. When is the balance due before departure? The balance — 70% of the charter price — is due 7 calendar days before your departure date. We send a reminder with the same payment link or IBAN you used for the deposit. If you book inside that 7-day window, the full price is settled on confirmation. Paying late risks losing the slot during peak July-August weekends when waiting lists fill the s

TITLE: Marbella Dolphin Watching: Morning vs Afternoon Yacht Charter Guide
DESCRIPTION: Compare morning vs afternoon yacht charters for dolphin watching in Marbella. Best times, sea conditions, pricing &amp; boat options from Puerto Banús.

Home › Guide › Marbella Dolphin Watching: Morning vs Afternoon Yacht Charter Guide By Andra Kiirkivi Founder & CEO · Boat Rental Marbella · Updated 20 May 2026 · Reviewed by local Marbella skippers Marbella’s coastline is one of the best places in the Mediterranean for dolphin watching, with common bottlenose and striped dolphins spotted year-round. But the time of day you choose for your yacht charter can change everything—from sea conditions and wildlife activity to the overall vibe. This guide compares morning and afternoon departures from Puerto Banús, so you can pick the slot that fits your group. Why timing matters for dolphin watching Dolphins are most active during feeding times, which often coincide with calmer seas. In Marbella, the sea breeze typically picks up around midday, making mornings (9am–1pm) generally flatter and more favourable for spotting dorsal fins. Afternoon charters (2pm–8pm) can still be excellent, especially if you prioritise sunset views and warmer swimming conditions. Morning charters: calm seas, active dolphins Morning trips from Puerto Banús head east towards Cabopino or west towards Estepona. The water is glassy, and dolphins often surface closer to shore. A 2-hour charter on the Astondoa 40 (€749/2h) is ideal for a quick sightseeing run. You’ll have the best light for photography, and the cooler air (around 24°C in summer) keeps everyone fresh. Morning charters also leave your afternoon free for other plans. Afternoon charters: sunset, warmth, and relaxation Afternoon departures let you combine dolphin watching with a swim stop at Cala del Faro or Cala Cortés, where the water has warmed to 25–26°C. The light turns golden by 6pm, making for spectacular dolphin silhouettes. The Mangusta 80 (€4,719/4h) is a favourite for groups wanting speed, space, and a free jet ski. Afternoon trips often feel more leisurely, with drinks flowing as the sun dips behind La Concha. Which boat to choose for your dolphin charter Boat Length Pax Price (2h / 4h) Best for Astondoa 40 12.5m 9 €749 / €2,299 Small groups, morning trips Azimut 39 12.5m 11 €749 / €2,299 Families, value Mangusta 80 24m 12 €4,719 (4h min) Large groups, jet ski add-on Pershing 46 14m 10 On request Speed, luxury All charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, cava, light snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT. No hidden costs. Sea conditions and dolphin behaviour Morning seas in Marbella are typically Beaufort scale 1–2 (calm to light ripples). By 2pm, the thermal wind often pushes conditions to Beaufort 3–4 (small waves, occasional whitecaps). Dolphins prefer calmer water for hunting, so morning trips statistically yield more sightings. That said, afternoons are far from useless—dolphins still appear, and the trade-off is a more relaxed pace with a swim stop. Best spots for dolphin watching near Marbella Cala del Faro – 20 minutes from Puerto Banús; common bottlenose dolphin territory. Cabo Pino – Shallow waters attract feeding pods. Estepona – Westward route; often sees striped dolphins. Río Verde – Close to Banús; good for short 2-hour trips. Pricing comparison: morning vs afternoon There’s no price difference between morning and afternoon for our fixed-rate boats. The Astondoa 40 costs €749 for 2 hours regardless of the slot. The Mangusta 80 is €4,719 for a minimum 4 hours. For other yachts like the Ferretti 94 or Canados 86, pricing is on request via WhatsApp. The real difference is value: morning trips pack more wildlife action into a shorter time; afternoon trips give you sunset and swimming. Highly recommend morning.” – family of 5, July 2024. “Afternoon trip on the Mangusta 80 was incredible. Dolphins at 5pm, then we swam and watched the sunset with cava. Perfect.” – group of 10, August 2024. Frequently asked questions What time of day is best for dolphin watching in Marbella? Morning charters (departing 9–10am) generally offer calmer seas and more active dolphins, especially in summer. Afternoon trips (2–3pm) can still yield sightings but may face stronger winds. Both times have success rates above 80% during peak season. How long should a dolphin-watching charter be? A 2-hour charter is enough to reach known dolphin areas like Cala del Faro or Cabopino. For a more relaxed experience, a 4-hour trip allows time to anchor, swim, and enjoy snacks while waiting for dolphins. Which boat is best for dolphin watching? The Astondoa 40 (€749/2h, 9 pax) is a popular choice for small groups. For larger parties, the Mangusta 80 (€4,719/4h, 12 pax) offers speed and comfort with a free jet ski. All boats have shaded areas and open bows for viewing. Do you guarantee dolphin sightings? No, we cannot guarantee sightings as dolphins are wild animals. However, our skippers know the best spots and timing. Sightings occur on roughly 85% of trips during summer. If you don't see any, you still enjoy a luxury cruise with drinks and swimming. What should I bring for a dolphin-watching charter? Bring sunscreen, sunglasses, a hat, a light jacket (for morning trips), swimwear, and a camera. We provide water, soft drinks, beer, cava, and light snacks. Towels and snorkel gear are available on request. Is the afternoon charter better for sunset? Yes, afternoon charters (e.g., 2–8pm) end around sunset, offering stunning views over the Mediterranean and La Concha mountain. Dolphins are sometimes seen silhouetted against the golden light, making for memorable photos. Can I combine dolphin watching with swimming? Absolutely. Most charters include stops at coves like Cala Cortés or Cala del Faro for swimming. The Mangusta 80 even includes a free jet ski. Afternoon trips often feature a longer swim stop because the water is warmer. How do I book a dolphin-watching charter? You can book directly via WhatsApp. For Tier A boats like Astondoa 40, pricing is fixed at €749/2h. For larger yachts, contact us for a custom quote.
